Underweight? I mean... look, I know everyone is making a massive deal out of my weight right now. But I still jog every single morning, I'm out there working my territory, hitting all my numbers... so it really doesn't feel like some sort of medical emergency to me, you know? 

Speaking of my territory, actually, they just expanded my route up into the northern counties last month. Do you have any idea how much driving that is? I had to trade in my truck for a hybrid sedan just to get decent gas mileage. The truck was just killing me at the pump. Gas prices are absolutely insane right now, don't you think? I mean, it eats right into your commission if you're not careful, and in wholesale, margins are already tight enough as it is. I had a client last week try to negotiate a ten percent discount just because of shipping costs. 

But anyway, yeah, I'm functioning perfectly fine. I'm still out there making the sales, so I really don't get why they think I'm so fragile all of a sudden.

Normal? I mean, what is normal anyway, right? Like I said, I know everyone is making a huge deal about it, but honestly... I'm out there every day. I jog every morning, rain or shine. I'm working my entire territory, hitting all my sales numbers. So no, it really doesn't feel like some sort of medical emergency to me. 

Actually, speaking of jogging, I just bought these new running shoes. The guy at the store swore they were the best for shock absorption, but I swear my left knee has been acting up ever since I started wearing them. Have you ever tried those ones with the really thick soles? They look a bit ridiculous, honestly, like you're walking on marshmallows, but people swear by them. I might have to go back and exchange them, but finding the time to get to the mall is just impossible right now with the new route. The traffic on I-95 is a nightmare past 3 PM. But yeah, as far as my weight goes, I'm functioning fine. I don't see the problem.

Afraid? I mean... yeah, I guess I am. If I step on the scale and it's even a single pound higher after a client dinner or a weekend out in Miami, I get this intense panic. Excessive? No, not really. I mean, I'm pretty careful about what I eat, so I don't really have those moments where I feel like I've just eaten way too much. I keep things pretty tight.

Lost control? No, never. I don't really do that. Like I said before, I keep things pretty tight. I'm actually really good at staying in control of things. You kind of have to be in my line of work, honestly. If you lose control of your schedule or your territory, you're just dead in the water. 

Speaking of which, I had my route mapped out perfectly yesterday—three big client visits, back-to-back. But then my car started making this weird rattling noise right under the steering column. I ended up having to take it into the dealership, and you wouldn't believe it. They tried to tell me I needed a whole new suspension system. I mean, it's a 2019 hybrid, it shouldn't need that yet, right? I told the service guy, look, I drive for a living, I know when a car is actually pulling or when it's just a loose piece of plastic. He just gave me this blank stare. 

It's impossible to find an honest mechanic these days. My dad used to have this guy, Sal, who would fix literally anything for fifty bucks and a six-pack, but Sal retired to Boca a few years ago. Now it's all these giant corporate service centers with waiting rooms that smell like cheap vanilla air freshener and stale coffee. They tried to charge me a hundred and twenty dollars just for the diagnostic fee! I ended up just leaving. I'll figure it out myself or turn the radio up louder, you know? But yeah, no, eating-wise, I'm always in control.

Yeah, I definitely worry about that. I mean, when I look in the mirror before a big pitch or a date or something, I just zero right in on my stomach and my face. If I think I look even a little bit soft, I just feel like people are going to see me as sloppy or weak. And you can't look weak in sales, you know? 

But man, speaking of dates, I actually went on one last Thursday. Met this girl on Hinge. We went to this upscale place downtown, and the parking was an absolute nightmare. I circled the block for twenty minutes before I finally found a spot, and the meter was broken. You know those new smart meters the city just installed? Half of them don't even read your credit card right. So I'm standing there in the freezing rain, trying to get the parking app to download on my phone, but my cell service was completely dead because of all the high-rises. 

By the time I got inside the restaurant, my shoes were completely soaked. They're these Italian leather loafers, really nice ones I got at Nordstrom Rack, and water just absolutely ruins that kind of leather. I tried stuffing them with newspaper the next day, but they still have that weird watermark right on the toe. Have you ever tried to get water stains out of tan leather? It's basically impossible. I might just have to take them to a cobbler, but who even knows a good cobbler anymore? It's a dying art. I looked on Yelp and the closest one with decent reviews is like forty-five minutes away in the suburbs. I just don't have the time to drive all the way out there for a pair of shoes.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, absolutely. I definitely restrict what I eat. It's like... if I step on the scale and it's even a pound higher after a client dinner or, you know, a weekend out in Miami, I get this intense panic. It feels like I'm completely losing control, and right away I start planning how I'm going to cut back the next day. I just can't handle the idea of gaining weight.

Speaking of Miami, though, I was just down there last month for a trade show, and the airport situation was insane. MIA is always a mess, but this time, the security line wrapped all the way back to the ticketing counters. And of course, I get stuck behind this family of like, six people, and none of them knew they had to take their laptops out of their bags. I'm standing there, looking at my watch, sweating through my dress shirt because they keep the AC way too low in that terminal. 

And then, when I finally get through, the tram to the gates was broken. So I had to sprint—literally sprint—in my suit, carrying my sample case, all the way to gate D60. Have you ever tried running half a mile with a twenty-pound case of hardware samples? My shoulder was killing me for a week. And the worst part? I get to the gate, completely out of breath, and they announce a two-hour delay because of thunderstorms over Orlando. I ended up just sitting at one of those overpriced airport bars, drinking sparkling water and paying fourteen dollars for it. It's ridiculous how much they mark things up once you're past security. They really just have a captive audience, so they can charge whatever they want. It's a brilliant business model, honestly, but as a consumer, it's infuriating.

I mean, when I look in the mirror before a big pitch or a date or something, I just zero right in on my stomach and my face. If I think I look even a little bit soft, I just feel like people are going to see me as sloppy or weak. We went to this upscale place downtown, and the parking was an absolute nightmare. I circled the block for twenty minutes before I finally found a spot, and the meter was broken. You know those new smart meters the city just installed? Half of them don't even read your credit card right. So I'm standing there in the freezing rain, trying to get the parking app to download on my phone, but my cell service was completely dead because of all the high-rises. \n\nBy the time I got inside the restaurant, my shoes were completely soaked. They're these Italian leather loafers, really nice ones I got at Nordstrom Rack, and water just absolutely ruins that kind of leather. I tried stuffing them with newspaper the next day, but they still have that weird watermark right on the toe. Have you ever tried to get water stains out of tan leather? It's basically impossible. I might just have to take them to a cobbler, but who even knows a good cobbler anymore? It's a dying art. I looked on Yelp and the closest one with decent reviews is like forty-five minutes away in the suburbs. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don't know, I guess... it's hard to say if it's the exact same. I just know that when I look in the mirror, I always see things I need to fix. Like, I always feel like I look heavier than I should, or just... not sharp enough, you know? It's like I can never quite get it right, no matter what the scale says. 

But speaking of getting things right, I was trying to fix this leaky faucet in my guest bathroom this weekend. I figured, how hard could it be, right? I watched a couple of YouTube videos, went down to Home Depot, bought all the washers and the plumber's tape. But the guy at the hardware store gave me the wrong size O-ring. I didn't realize it until I had the whole sink taken apart, water shut off, tools everywhere. 

So I had to drive all the way back to the store, and of course, it's a Saturday afternoon, so the place is an absolute zoo. You can't find an employee to help you to save your life. I spent twenty minutes wandering up and down the plumbing aisle just looking for this tiny little piece of rubber. And then when I finally get back home and put it all together, it still drips! Just this slow, annoying drip... drip... drip. It drives me crazy when I'm trying to sleep. I think I'm just going to have to bite the bullet and call a professional, but plumbers charge like, a hundred and fifty bucks just to walk through the door these days. It's highway robbery.

Have you noticed how much everything costs lately?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that feels excessive to you?\n\nPatient: Excessive? No, not really. I mean, I'm pretty careful about what I eat, so I don't really have those moments where I feel like I've just eaten way too much. I keep things pretty tight. \n\nSpeaking of keeping things tight, though, I've been trying to organize my home office lately. I have all these product catalogs and samples just piling up everywhere. I bought this new filing cabinet off Amazon, right? Supposed to be heavy-duty steel, industrial grade. It arrived with a huge dent in the side. The delivery guy just left it on my porch, didn't even ring the doorbell. Have you ever tried returning something that big? It's a total nightmare. You have to print the label, figure out how to box it back up, and then haul it to the UPS store. I drive that hybrid now, remember? Trying to fit a four-drawer filing cabinet into the back of a sedan is practically a comedy sketch. I ended up just keeping it and putting it in the corner where the dent doesn't show as much. But still, it's the principle of the thing, you know? You pay for something new, you expect it to be new. Retail customer service is just not what it used to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Lost control? No, never. I don't really do that. Like I said before, I keep things pretty tight. I'm actually really good at staying in control of things. You kind of have to be in my line of work, honestly. If you lose control of your schedule or your territory, you're just dead in the water. \n\nSpeaking of which, I had my route mapped out perfectly yesterday—three big client visits, back-to-back. But then my car started making this weird rattling noise right under the steering column. I ended up having to take it into the dealership, and you wouldn't believe it. They tried to tell me I needed a whole new suspension system. I mean, it's a 2019 hybrid, it shouldn't need that yet, right? I told the service guy, look, I drive for a living, I know when a car is actually pulling or when it's just a loose piece of plastic. He just gave me this blank stare. \n\nIt's impossible to find an honest mechanic these days. My dad used to have this guy, Sal, who would fix literally anything for fifty bucks and a six-pack, but Sal retired to Boca a few years ago. Now it's all these giant corporate service centers with waiting rooms that smell like cheap vanilla air freshener and stale coffee. They tried to charge me a hundred and twenty dollars just for the diagnostic fee! I ended up just leaving. I'll figure it out myself or turn the radio up louder, you know? But yeah, no, eating-wise, I'm always in control.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about your appearance when you think about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely worry about that. I mean, when I look in the mirror before a big pitch or a date or something, I just zero right in on my stomach and my face. If I think I look even a little bit soft, I just feel like people are going to see me as sloppy or weak. And you can't look weak in sales, you know? \n\nBut man, speaking of dates, I actually went on one last Thursday. Met this girl on Hinge. We went to this upscale place downtown, and the parking was an absolute nightmare. I circled the block for twenty minutes before I finally found a spot, and the meter was broken. You know those new smart meters the city just installed? Half of them don't even read your credit card right. So I'm standing there in the freezing rain, trying to get the parking app to download on my phone, but my cell service was completely dead because of all the high-rises. \n\nBy the time I got inside the restaurant, my shoes were completely soaked. They're these Italian leather loafers, really nice ones I got at Nordstrom Rack, and water just absolutely ruins that kind of leather. I tried stuffing them with newspaper the next day, but they still have that weird watermark right on the toe. Have you ever tried to get water stains out of tan leather? It's basically impossible. I might just have to take them to a cobbler, but who even knows a good cobbler anymore? It's a dying art. I looked on Yelp and the closest one with decent reviews is like forty-five minutes away in the suburbs. I just don't have the time to drive all the way out there for a pair of shoes.\n\nDoctor: Do you restrict what you eat or how much you eat because you're afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, absolutely. I definitely restrict what I eat. It's like... if I step on the scale and it's even a pound higher after a client dinner or, you know, a weekend out in Miami, I get this intense panic. It feels like I'm completely losing control, and right away I start planning how I'm going to cut back the next day. I just can't handle the idea of gaining weight.\n\nSpeaking of Miami, though, I was just down there last month for a trade show, and the airport situation was insane. My shoulder was killing me for a week. And the worst part? I get to the gate, completely out of breath, and they announce a two-hour delay because of thunderstorms over Orlando. I ended up just sitting at one of those overpriced airport bars, drinking sparkling water and paying fourteen dollars for it. It's ridiculous how much they mark things up once you're past security. They really just have a captive audience, so they can charge whatever they want. It's a brilliant business model, honestly, but as a consumer, it's infuriating.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the same as what you see when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I don't know, I guess... it's hard to say if it's the exact same. I mean, I know everyone else is making a huge deal about it. My family, some people at work... they all act like I'm too thin or whatever. But honestly, it just doesn't feel like some big medical emergency to me. I'm still out there jogging every single morning, I'm working my entire territory, and I'm hitting all my sales numbers. But speaking of jogging, I actually changed up my route yesterday and ran through that new subdivision they're building out past Route 9. Have you seen those places? It's all that fake stone veneer on the front, and they look nice from the street, but I was talking to a guy I know who does contracting, and he said the materials they're using are just absolute garbage. Like, they use this cheap composite wood that warps as soon as it gets humid. 

And don't even get me started on the HOAs out there. My sister lives in a development just like that, and they literally sent her a fifty-dollar fine last month because her mailbox was the wrong shade of beige. Not even a different color, just the wrong exact shade of beige! Who even has the time to walk around with a color swatch checking people's mailboxes? It's completely out of control. I told her she should just sell the place and move, but with the housing market the way it is right now, and interest rates being over seven percent, you basically can't afford to move even if you want to. It's just a terrible time to be a buyer.

But yeah, as far as my weight goes, I'm functioning fine. I don't see the problem.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Afraid? I mean... yeah, I guess I am. If I step on the scale and it's even a single pound higher after a client dinner or a weekend out in Miami, I get this intense panic. South Beach. The hotel prices down there are absolute extortion right now. They hit my corporate card for forty-five bucks a night just for valet parking! Can you believe that? She’s been raising her prices lately, too, which is a whole other headache. It’s like everyone is just nickel and diming you these days. Inflation is really doing a number on our expense accounts, let me tell you. My regional manager actually called me out on my travel report last week because of it. Have you noticed how much everything costs lately?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that feels excessive to you?\n\nPatient: Excessive? No, not really. I mean, I'm pretty careful about what I eat, so I don't really have those moments where I feel like I've just eaten way too much. I keep things pretty tight. \n\nSpeaking of keeping things tight, though, I've been trying to organize my home office lately. I have all these product catalogs and samples just piling up everywhere. I bought this new filing cabinet off Amazon, right? Supposed to be heavy-duty steel, industrial grade. It arrived with a huge dent in the side. The delivery guy just left it on my porch, didn't even ring the doorbell. Have you ever tried returning something that big? It's a total nightmare. You have to print the label, figure out how to box it back up, and then haul it to the UPS store. I drive that hybrid now, remember? Trying to fit a four-drawer filing cabinet into the back of a sedan is practically a comedy sketch. I ended up just keeping it and putting it in the corner where the dent doesn't show as much. But still, it's the principle of the thing, you know? You pay for something new, you expect it to be new. Retail customer service is just not what it used to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Lost control? No, never. I don't really do that. Like I said before, I keep things pretty tight. I'm actually really good at staying in control of things. You kind of have to be in my line of work, honestly. If you lose control of your schedule or your territory, you're just dead in the water. \n\nSpeaking of which, I had my route mapped out perfectly yesterday—three big client visits, back-to-back. But then my car started making this weird rattling noise right under the steering column. I ended up having to take it into the dealership, and you wouldn't believe it. They tried to tell me I needed a whole new suspension system. I mean, it's a 2019 hybrid, it shouldn't need that yet, right? He just gave me this blank stare. \n\nIt's impossible to find an honest mechanic these days. I mean, when I look in the mirror before a big pitch or a date or something, I just zero right in on my stomach and my face. If I think I look even a little bit soft, I just feel like people are going to see me as sloppy or weak. I know we kind of just touched on this, but to be more specific... when I look in the mirror, especially right before a big client meeting or a date or something, I just immediately zero in on my stomach and my face. If I step on the scale and it's even a single pound higher after a client dinner or a weekend out in Miami, I get this intense panic. It just feels like I'm completely losing control, and I instantly start planning exactly how I'm going to cut back the next day. \n\nBut man, speaking of Miami, I was just down there for a trade show a couple weeks ago. South Beach. The hotel prices down there are absolute extortion right now. They hit my corporate card for forty-five bucks a night just for valet parking! Can you believe that? And the humidity is just brutal. It absolutely wrecks my suits. I had to take three of them straight to my dry cleaner, Mrs. Higgins, the second I flew back. She’s been raising her prices lately, too, which is a whole other headache. It’s like everyone is just nickel and diming you these days. Inflation is really doing a number on our expense accounts, let me tell you. My regional manager actually called me out on my travel report last week because of it. Have you noticed how much everything costs lately?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that feels excessive to you?\n\nPatient: Excessive? No, not really. I mean, I'm pretty careful about what I eat, so I don't really have those moments where I feel like I've just eaten way too much. I keep things pretty tight. \n\nSpeaking of keeping things tight, though, I've been trying to organize my home office lately. I have all these product catalogs and samples just piling up everywhere. I bought this new filing cabinet off Amazon, right? Supposed to be heavy-duty steel, industrial grade. It arrived with a huge dent in the side. The delivery guy just left it on my porch, didn't even ring the doorbell. Have you ever tried returning something that big? It's a total nightmare. You have to print the label, figure out how to box it back up, and then haul it to the UPS store. I drive that hybrid now, remember? Trying to fit a four-drawer filing cabinet into the back of a sedan is practically a comedy sketch. I ended up just keeping it and putting it in the corner where the dent doesn't show as much. But still, it's the principle of the thing, you know? You pay for something new, you expect it to be new. Retail customer service is just not what it used to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Lost control? No, never. I don't really do that. Like I said before, I keep things pretty tight. I'm actually really good at staying in control of things. You kind of have to be in my line of work, honestly. If you lose control of your schedule or your territory, you're just dead in the water. \n\nSpeaking of which, I had my route mapped out perfectly yesterday—three big client visits, back-to-back. But then my car started making this weird rattling noise right under the steering column. I ended up having to take it into the dealership, and you wouldn't believe it. They tried to tell me I needed a whole new suspension system. I mean, it's a 2019 hybrid, it shouldn't need that yet, right? I told the service guy, look, I drive for a living, I know when a car is actually pulling or when it's just a loose piece of plastic. He just gave me this blank stare. \n\nIt's impossible to find an honest mechanic these days. My dad used to have this guy, Sal, who would fix literally anything for fifty bucks and a six-pack, but Sal retired to Boca a few years ago. Now it's all these giant corporate service centers with waiting rooms that smell like cheap vanilla air freshener and stale coffee. They tried to charge me a hundred and twenty dollars just for the diagnostic fee! I ended up just leaving. I'll figure it out myself or turn the radio up louder, you know? But yeah, no, eating-wise, I'm always in control.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about your appearance when you think about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely worry about that. I mean, when I look in the mirror before a big pitch or a date or something, I just zero right in on my stomach and my face. If I think I look even a little bit soft, I just feel like people are going to see me as sloppy or weak. And you can't look weak in sales, you know? \n\nBut man, speaking of dates, I actually went on one last Thursday. Met this girl on Hinge. We went to this upscale place downtown, and the parking was an absolute nightmare. I circled the block for twenty minutes before I finally found a spot, and the meter was broken. You know those new smart meters the city just installed? Half of them don't even read your credit card right. So I'm standing there in the freezing rain, trying to get the parking app to download on my phone, but my cell service was completely dead because of all the high-rises. \n\nBy the time I got inside the restaurant, my shoes were completely soaked. They're these Italian leather loafers, really nice ones I got at Nordstrom Rack, and water just absolutely ruins that kind of leather. I tried stuffing them with newspaper the next day, but they still have that weird watermark right on the toe. Have you ever tried to get water stains out of tan leather? It's basically impossible. I might just have to take them to a cobbler, but who even knows a good cobbler anymore? It's a dying art. I looked on Yelp and the closest one with decent reviews is like forty-five minutes away in the suburbs. I just don't have the time to drive all the way out there for a pair of shoes.\n\nDoctor: Do you restrict what you eat or how much you eat because you're afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, absolutely. I definitely restrict what I eat. It's like... if I step on the scale and it's even a pound higher after a client dinner or, you know, a weekend out in Miami, I get this intense panic. It feels like I'm completely losing control, and right away I start planning how I'm going to cut back the next day. MIA is always a mess, but this time, the security line wrapped all the way back to the ticketing counters. And of course, I get stuck behind this family of like, six people, and none of them knew they had to take their laptops out of their bags. I'm standing there, looking at my watch, sweating through my dress shirt because they keep the AC way too low in that terminal. \n\nAnd then, when I finally get through, the tram to the gates was broken. So I had to sprint—literally sprint—in my suit, carrying my sample case, all the way to gate D60. Have you ever tried running half a mile with a twenty-pound case of hardware samples? My shoulder was killing me for a week. And the worst part? I get to the gate, completely out of breath, and they announce a two-hour delay because of thunderstorms over Orlando. I ended up just sitting at one of those overpriced airport bars, drinking sparkling water and paying fourteen dollars for it. It's ridiculous how much they mark things up once you're past security. They really just have a captive audience, so they can charge whatever they want. It's a brilliant business model, honestly, but as a consumer, it's infuriating.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the same as what you see when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I don't know, I guess... it's hard to say if it's the exact same. I just know that when I look in the mirror, I always see things I need to fix. Like, I always feel like I look heavier than I should, or just... not sharp enough, you know? It's like I can never quite get it right, no matter what the scale says. \n\nBut speaking of getting things right, I was trying to fix this leaky faucet in my guest bathroom this weekend. I figured, how hard could it be, right? I watched a couple of YouTube videos, went down to Home Depot, bought all the washers and the plumber's tape. But the guy at the hardware store gave me the wrong size O-ring. I didn't realize it until I had the whole sink taken apart, water shut off, tools everywhere. \n\nSo I had to drive all the way back to the store, and of course, it's a Saturday afternoon, so the place is an absolute zoo. You can't find an employee to help you to save your life. I spent twenty minutes wandering up and down the plumbing aisle just looking for this tiny little piece of rubber. And then when I finally get back home and put it all together, it still drips! Just this slow, annoying drip... drip... drip. It drives me crazy when I'm trying to sleep. I think I'm just going to have to bite the bullet and call a professional, but plumbers charge like, a hundred and fifty bucks just to walk through the door these days. It's highway robbery.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is lower than what other people think it is?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know everyone else is making a huge deal about it. Like, they use this cheap composite wood that warps as soon as it gets humid. \n\nAnd don't even get me started on the HOAs out there. My sister lives in a development just like that, and they literally sent her a fifty-dollar fine last month because her mailbox was the wrong shade of beige. Not even a different color, just the wrong exact shade of beige! Who even has the time to walk around with a color swatch checking people's mailboxes? It's completely out of control. I told her she should just sell the place and move, but with the housing market the way it is right now, and interest rates being over seven percent, you basically can't afford to move even if you want to. It's just a terrible time to be a buyer.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the same as what you see when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I know we kind of just touched on this, but to be more specific... when I look in the mirror, especially right before a big client meeting or a date or something, I just immediately zero in on my stomach and my face. I'm not really sure, honestly. I don't really think about it like that... like if the actual number is lower than what the reflection is showing me. I just see what's there, you know? 

But man, speaking of reflections, I was staring at myself in the mirror at the barber shop on Tuesday before a big client pitch, and... have you noticed how insane haircut prices have gotten lately? I go to this place downtown, just a standard old-school barber, nothing fancy, and they jacked the price up to forty-five dollars for a basic fade. Forty-five dollars! That's almost two hundred bucks an hour if you do the math. I should have just gone to barber school and made a killing doing that instead of wholesale. 

Plus, they always try to upsell you on those artisan beard oils and pomades that cost thirty bucks a pop. I actually bought one a few months ago because the guy wouldn't stop pushing it, and it smelled like pine needles and motor oil. It was awful. From my side, I just see that my suits fit sharper. Have you noticed how hard it is to find a good tailor these days? I used to go to this guy downtown, Emilio, but he closed up shop last year. Shame, really. Anyway, my weight has just kept dropping since the divorce. My ex-wife, she took the dog, you know? A golden retriever named Buster. I miss that dog more than her sometimes, if I'm being honest. But yeah, everyone's making a fuss about the weight, but I think I look great for a guy in sales. You gotta look sharp in wholesale, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are underweight?\n\nPatient: Underweight? I mean... look, I know everyone is making a massive deal out of my weight right now. But I still jog every single morning, I'm out there working my territory, hitting all my numbers... so it really doesn't feel like some sort of medical emergency to me, you know? \n\nSpeaking of my territory, actually, they just expanded my route up into the northern counties last month. Do you have any idea how much driving that is? I had to trade in my truck for a hybrid sedan just to get decent gas mileage. The truck was just killing me at the pump. Gas prices are absolutely insane right now, don't you think? I mean, it eats right into your commission if you're not careful, and in wholesale, margins are already tight enough as it is. I had a client last week try to negotiate a ten percent discount just because of shipping costs. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I'm functioning perfectly fine. I'm still out there making the sales, so I really don't get why they think I'm so fragile all of a sudden.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is normal for your height?\n\nPatient: Normal? I mean, what is normal anyway, right? Like I said, I know everyone is making a huge deal about it, but honestly... I'm out there every day. I jog every morning, rain or shine. I'm working my entire territory, hitting all my sales numbers. So no, it really doesn't feel like some sort of medical emergency to me. \n\nActually, speaking of jogging, I just bought these new running shoes. The guy at the store swore they were the best for shock absorption, but I swear my left knee has been acting up ever since I started wearing them. Have you ever tried those ones with the really thick soles? They look a bit ridiculous, honestly, like you're walking on marshmallows, but people swear by them. I might have to go back and exchange them, but finding the time to get to the mall is just impossible right now with the new route. The traffic on I-95 is a nightmare past 3 PM. But yeah, as far as my weight goes, I'm functioning fine. I don't see the problem.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Afraid? I mean... yeah, I guess I am. If I step on the scale and it's even a single pound higher after a client dinner or a weekend out in Miami, I get this intense panic. It just feels like I'm completely losing control, and I instantly start planning exactly how I'm going to cut back the next day. \n\nBut man, speaking of Miami, I was just down there for a trade show a couple weeks ago. South Beach. The hotel prices down there are absolute extortion right now. They hit my corporate card for forty-five bucks a night just for valet parking! Can you believe that? And the humidity is just brutal. It absolutely wrecks my suits. I had to take three of them straight to my dry cleaner, Mrs. Higgins, the second I flew back. She’s been raising her prices lately, too, which is a whole other headache. It’s like everyone is just nickel and diming you these days. Inflation is really doing a number on our expense accounts, let me tell you. My regional manager actually called me out on my travel report last week because of it. Have you noticed how much everything costs lately?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that feels excessive to you?\n\nPatient: Excessive? No, not really. I mean, I'm pretty careful about what I eat, so I don't really have those moments where I feel like I've just eaten way too much. I keep things pretty tight. \n\nSpeaking of keeping things tight, though, I've been trying to organize my home office lately. I have all these product catalogs and samples just piling up everywhere. I bought this new filing cabinet off Amazon, right? Supposed to be heavy-duty steel, industrial grade. It arrived with a huge dent in the side. The delivery guy just left it on my porch, didn't even ring the doorbell. Have you ever tried returning something that big? It's a total nightmare. You have to print the label, figure out how to box it back up, and then haul it to the UPS store. I drive that hybrid now, remember? Trying to fit a four-drawer filing cabinet into the back of a sedan is practically a comedy sketch. I ended up just keeping it and putting it in the corner where the dent doesn't show as much. But still, it's the principle of the thing, you know? You pay for something new, you expect it to be new. Retail customer service is just not what it used to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Lost control? No, never. I don't really do that. Like I said before, I keep things pretty tight. I'm actually really good at staying in control of things. You kind of have to be in my line of work, honestly. If you lose control of your schedule or your territory, you're just dead in the water. \n\nSpeaking of which, I had my route mapped out perfectly yesterday—three big client visits, back-to-back. But then my car started making this weird rattling noise right under the steering column. I ended up having to take it into the dealership, and you wouldn't believe it. They tried to tell me I needed a whole new suspension system. I mean, it's a 2019 hybrid, it shouldn't need that yet, right? I told the service guy, look, I drive for a living, I know when a car is actually pulling or when it's just a loose piece of plastic. He just gave me this blank stare. \n\nIt's impossible to find an honest mechanic these days. My dad used to have this guy, Sal, who would fix literally anything for fifty bucks and a six-pack, but Sal retired to Boca a few years ago. Now it's all these giant corporate service centers with waiting rooms that smell like cheap vanilla air freshener and stale coffee. They tried to charge me a hundred and twenty dollars just for the diagnostic fee! I ended up just leaving. I'll figure it out myself or turn the radio up louder, you know? But yeah, no, eating-wise, I'm always in control.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about your appearance when you think about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely worry about that. I mean, when I look in the mirror before a big pitch or a date or something, I just zero right in on my stomach and my face. If I think I look even a little bit soft, I just feel like people are going to see me as sloppy or weak. And you can't look weak in sales, you know? \n\nBut man, speaking of dates, I actually went on one last Thursday. Met this girl on Hinge. We went to this upscale place downtown, and the parking was an absolute nightmare. I circled the block for twenty minutes before I finally found a spot, and the meter was broken. You know those new smart meters the city just installed? Half of them don't even read your credit card right. So I'm standing there in the freezing rain, trying to get the parking app to download on my phone, but my cell service was completely dead because of all the high-rises. \n\nBy the time I got inside the restaurant, my shoes were completely soaked. They're these Italian leather loafers, really nice ones I got at Nordstrom Rack, and water just absolutely ruins that kind of leather. I tried stuffing them with newspaper the next day, but they still have that weird watermark right on the toe. Have you ever tried to get water stains out of tan leather? It's basically impossible. I might just have to take them to a cobbler, but who even knows a good cobbler anymore? It's a dying art. I looked on Yelp and the closest one with decent reviews is like forty-five minutes away in the suburbs. I just don't have the time to drive all the way out there for a pair of shoes.\n\nDoctor: Do you restrict what you eat or how much you eat because you're afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, absolutely. I definitely restrict what I eat. It's like... if I step on the scale and it's even a pound higher after a client dinner or, you know, a weekend out in Miami, I get this intense panic. It feels like I'm completely losing control, and right away I start planning how I'm going to cut back the next day. I just can't handle the idea of gaining weight.\n\nSpeaking of Miami, though, I was just down there last month for a trade show, and the airport situation was insane. MIA is always a mess, but this time, the security line wrapped all the way back to the ticketing counters. And of course, I get stuck behind this family of like, six people, and none of them knew they had to take their laptops out of their bags. I'm standing there, looking at my watch, sweating through my dress shirt because they keep the AC way too low in that terminal. \n\nAnd then, when I finally get through, the tram to the gates was broken. So I had to sprint—literally sprint—in my suit, carrying my sample case, all the way to gate D60. Have you ever tried running half a mile with a twenty-pound case of hardware samples? My shoulder was killing me for a week. And the worst part? I get to the gate, completely out of breath, and they announce a two-hour delay because of thunderstorms over Orlando. I ended up just sitting at one of those overpriced airport bars, drinking sparkling water and paying fourteen dollars for it. It's ridiculous how much they mark things up once you're past security. They really just have a captive audience, so they can charge whatever they want. It's a brilliant business model, honestly, but as a consumer, it's infuriating.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the same as what you see when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I don't know, I guess... it's hard to say if it's the exact same. I just know that when I look in the mirror, I always see things I need to fix. Like, I always feel like I look heavier than I should, or just... not sharp enough, you know? It's like I can never quite get it right, no matter what the scale says. \n\nBut speaking of getting things right, I was trying to fix this leaky faucet in my guest bathroom this weekend. I figured, how hard could it be, right? I watched a couple of YouTube videos, went down to Home Depot, bought all the washers and the plumber's tape. But the guy at the hardware store gave me the wrong size O-ring. I didn't realize it until I had the whole sink taken apart, water shut off, tools everywhere. \n\nSo I had to drive all the way back to the store, and of course, it's a Saturday afternoon, so the place is an absolute zoo. You can't find an employee to help you to save your life. I spent twenty minutes wandering up and down the plumbing aisle just looking for this tiny little piece of rubber. And then when I finally get back home and put it all together, it still drips! Just this slow, annoying drip... drip... drip. It drives me crazy when I'm trying to sleep. I think I'm just going to have to bite the bullet and call a professional, but plumbers charge like, a hundred and fifty bucks just to walk through the door these days. It's highway robbery.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is lower than what other people think it is?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know everyone else is making a huge deal about it. My family, some people at work... they all act like I'm too thin or whatever. But honestly, it just doesn't feel like some big medical emergency to me. I'm still out there jogging every single morning, I'm working my entire territory, and I'm hitting all my sales numbers. If I was really that bad off, I wouldn't be able to do all that, right? \n\nBut speaking of jogging, I actually changed up my route yesterday and ran through that new subdivision they're building out past Route 9. Have you seen those places? They're throwing up these massive houses with basically zero yard space. It's all that fake stone veneer on the front, and they look nice from the street, but I was talking to a guy I know who does contracting, and he said the materials they're using are just absolute garbage. Like, they use this cheap composite wood that warps as soon as it gets humid. \n\nAnd don't even get me started on the HOAs out there. My sister lives in a development just like that, and they literally sent her a fifty-dollar fine last month because her mailbox was the wrong shade of beige. Not even a different color, just the wrong exact shade of beige! Who even has the time to walk around with a color swatch checking people's mailboxes? It's completely out of control. I told her she should just sell the place and move, but with the housing market the way it is right now, and interest rates being over seven percent, you basically can't afford to move even if you want to. It's just a terrible time to be a buyer.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the same as what you see when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I know we kind of just touched on this, but to be more specific... when I look in the mirror, especially right before a big client meeting or a date or something, I just immediately zero in on my stomach and my face. If I think I look even a little bit soft, I panic. I just feel like people are going to look at me and think I'm sloppy or weak, you know? In sales, perception is everything. \n\nBut speaking of dates, I actually went on one last Thursday to that new seafood place down by the waterfront. Have you been down there since they finished the construction? It's an absolute nightmare. They completely got rid of all the street parking, so you're forced to use their valet service. And the valet is like, thirty dollars! Just to park your car for an hour and a half. Plus, you have to tip the guy, obviously. \n\nAnd then we're sitting there waiting for the car afterward, and it just starts pouring rain out of nowhere. The valet guys are just standing under the awning, not even hustling to get the cars. I was standing there getting misted on, just thinking about how my leather shoes were getting ruined. They're these Italian loafers I picked up on a business trip to Chicago last year, and water just completely destroys that kind of leather. I had to spend an hour on Sunday just cleaning and conditioning them with this special wax. It's just ridiculous how these places force you to pay premium prices for terrible service. I'm definitely going to leave a Yelp review about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is lower than what you actually see when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, honestly. I don't really think about it like that... like if the actual number is lower than what the reflection is showing me. I just see what's there, you know? \n\nBut man, speaking of reflections, I was staring at myself in the mirror at the barber shop on Tuesday before a big client pitch, and... have you noticed how insane haircut prices have gotten lately? I go to this place downtown, just a standard old-school barber, nothing fancy, and they jacked the price up to forty-five dollars for a basic fade. Forty-five dollars! And that's not even including the tip. You sit down in front of those huge mirrors they have, and the guy spends maybe fifteen, twenty minutes on your head. That's almost two hundred bucks an hour if you do the math. I should have just gone to barber school and made a killing doing that instead of wholesale. \n\nPlus, they always try to upsell you on those artisan beard oils and pomades that cost thirty bucks a pop. I actually bought one a few months ago because the guy wouldn't stop pushing it, and it smelled like pine needles and motor oil. It was awful. I put it on once before a sales call and I swear the buyer was leaning away from me the whole time. I had to throw the whole tin in the trash. It's just a total racket if you ask me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I feel like we just talked about this, but yeah... I don't know if I look *different* exactly, but when I look in the mirror, especially right before a big client meeting or a date or something, I just immediately zero in on my stomach and my face. I ended up ordering one online, but now I have to wait three weeks for it to ship because it's coming from overseas. And with the way shipping is going right now, like I was saying earlier, I'll probably be sitting on that dining chair until Christmas. My lower back is just screaming by 3 PM every day. I've started taking my afternoon calls standing up in the kitchen just to get some relief.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean, I don't really know. I can't read their minds. I know they tell me I look skinny, but I don't know if they see the same... the same stuff I see when I look at myself. It's hard to say. 

But you know, talking about how people see things... I was at the grocery store last night, and the way they've reorganized the aisles is just completely baffling. They moved the coffee. Who moves the coffee? It's been in aisle four since I moved to this neighborhood five years ago. Now it's tucked away in some weird corner next to the baking supplies. I spent twenty minutes wandering around like an idiot looking for my usual dark roast. 

And don't even get me started on the self-checkout lines. They installed these new machines that are supposed to be faster, but they're so sensitive that if you put your bags down slightly off-center, the whole thing freezes up and starts flashing a red light. Then you have to stand there waiting for the one employee who's managing twelve registers to come over and scan her badge. I just wanted a bag of coffee and some apples, and it turned into a thirty-minute ordeal. I swear, the next time I need groceries, I'm just going to pay the delivery fee and let someone else deal with it. My time is worth more than the ten bucks they charge to bring it to my door.

But I still jog every single morning, I'm out there working my territory, hitting all my numbers... so it really doesn't feel like some sort of medical emergency to me, you know? \n\nSpeaking of my territory, actually, they just expanded my route up into the northern counties last month. Do you have any idea how much driving that is? I had to trade in my truck for a hybrid sedan just to get decent gas mileage. The truck was just killing me at the pump. Gas prices are absolutely insane right now, don't you think? I mean, it eats right into your commission if you're not careful, and in wholesale, margins are already tight enough as it is. I had a client last week try to negotiate a ten percent discount just because of shipping costs. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I'm functioning perfectly fine. I'm still out there making the sales, so I really don't get why they think I'm so fragile all of a sudden.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is normal for your height?\n\nPatient: Normal? I mean, what is normal anyway, right? Like I said, I know everyone is making a huge deal about it, but honestly... I'm out there every day. I jog every morning, rain or shine. I'm working my entire territory, hitting all my sales numbers. So no, it really doesn't feel like some sort of medical emergency to me. \n\nActually, speaking of jogging, I just bought these new running shoes. The guy at the store swore they were the best for shock absorption, but I swear my left knee has been acting up ever since I started wearing them. Have you ever tried those ones with the really thick soles? They look a bit ridiculous, honestly, like you're walking on marshmallows, but people swear by them. I might have to go back and exchange them, but finding the time to get to the mall is just impossible right now with the new route. The traffic on I-95 is a nightmare past 3 PM. But yeah, as far as my weight goes, I'm functioning fine. I don't see the problem.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Afraid? I mean... yeah, I guess I am. If I step on the scale and it's even a single pound higher after a client dinner or a weekend out in Miami, I get this intense panic. It just feels like I'm completely losing control, and I instantly start planning exactly how I'm going to cut back the next day. \n\nBut man, speaking of Miami, I was just down there for a trade show a couple weeks ago. South Beach. The hotel prices down there are absolute extortion right now. They hit my corporate card for forty-five bucks a night just for valet parking! Can you believe that? And the humidity is just brutal. It absolutely wrecks my suits. I had to take three of them straight to my dry cleaner, Mrs. Higgins, the second I flew back. She’s been raising her prices lately, too, which is a whole other headache. It’s like everyone is just nickel and diming you these days. Inflation is really doing a number on our expense accounts, let me tell you. My regional manager actually called me out on my travel report last week because of it. Have you noticed how much everything costs lately?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that feels excessive to you?\n\nPatient: Excessive? No, not really. I mean, I'm pretty careful about what I eat, so I don't really have those moments where I feel like I've just eaten way too much. I keep things pretty tight. \n\nSpeaking of keeping things tight, though, I've been trying to organize my home office lately. I have all these product catalogs and samples just piling up everywhere. I bought this new filing cabinet off Amazon, right? Supposed to be heavy-duty steel, industrial grade. It arrived with a huge dent in the side. The delivery guy just left it on my porch, didn't even ring the doorbell. Have you ever tried returning something that big? It's a total nightmare. Trying to fit a four-drawer filing cabinet into the back of a sedan is practically a comedy sketch. I ended up just keeping it and putting it in the corner where the dent doesn't show as much. Retail customer service is just not what it used to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Lost control? No, never. I don't really do that. Like I said before, I keep things pretty tight. I'm actually really good at staying in control of things. You kind of have to be in my line of work, honestly. If you lose control of your schedule or your territory, you're just dead in the water. \n\nSpeaking of which, I had my route mapped out perfectly yesterday—three big client visits, back-to-back. But then my car started making this weird rattling noise right under the steering column. I ended up having to take it into the dealership, and you wouldn't believe it. They tried to tell me I needed a whole new suspension system. I mean, it's a 2019 hybrid, it shouldn't need that yet, right? I told the service guy, look, I drive for a living, I know when a car is actually pulling or when it's just a loose piece of plastic. He just gave me this blank stare. \n\nIt's impossible to find an honest mechanic these days. My dad used to have this guy, Sal, who would fix literally anything for fifty bucks and a six-pack, but Sal retired to Boca a few years ago. Now it's all these giant corporate service centers with waiting rooms that smell like cheap vanilla air freshener and stale coffee. They tried to charge me a hundred and twenty dollars just for the diagnostic fee! I ended up just leaving. I'll figure it out myself or turn the radio up louder, you know? But yeah, no, eating-wise, I'm always in control.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about your appearance when you think about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely worry about that. I mean, when I look in the mirror before a big pitch or a date or something, I just zero right in on my stomach and my face. If I think I look even a little bit soft, I just feel like people are going to see me as sloppy or weak. We went to this upscale place downtown, and the parking was an absolute nightmare. I circled the block for twenty minutes before I finally found a spot, and the meter was broken. You know those new smart meters the city just installed? Half of them don't even read your credit card right. So I'm standing there in the freezing rain, trying to get the parking app to download on my phone, but my cell service was completely dead because of all the high-rises. \n\nBy the time I got inside the restaurant, my shoes were completely soaked. I tried stuffing them with newspaper the next day, but they still have that weird watermark right on the toe. Have you ever tried to get water stains out of tan leather? It's basically impossible. I might just have to take them to a cobbler, but who even knows a good cobbler anymore? It's a dying art. I looked on Yelp and the closest one with decent reviews is like forty-five minutes away in the suburbs. I just don't have the time to drive all the way out there for a pair of shoes.\n\nDoctor: Do you restrict what you eat or how much you eat because you're afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, absolutely. I definitely restrict what I eat. It's like... if I step on the scale and it's even a pound higher after a client dinner or, you know, a weekend out in Miami, I get this intense panic. It's highway robbery.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is lower than what other people think it is?\n\nPatient: I mean, I know everyone else is making a huge deal about it. My family, some people at work... they all act like I'm too thin or whatever. But honestly, it just doesn't feel like some big medical emergency to me. I'm still out there jogging every single morning, I'm working my entire territory, and I'm hitting all my sales numbers. Have you seen those places? They're throwing up these massive houses with basically zero yard space. It's all that fake stone veneer on the front, and they look nice from the street, but I was talking to a guy I know who does contracting, and he said the materials they're using are just absolute garbage. Like, they use this cheap composite wood that warps as soon as it gets humid. \n\nAnd don't even get me started on the HOAs out there. My sister lives in a development just like that, and they literally sent her a fifty-dollar fine last month because her mailbox was the wrong shade of beige. Not even a different color, just the wrong exact shade of beige! Who even has the time to walk around with a color swatch checking people's mailboxes? It's completely out of control. I told her she should just sell the place and move, but with the housing market the way it is right now, and interest rates being over seven percent, you basically can't afford to move even if you want to. That's almost two hundred bucks an hour if you do the math. I should have just gone to barber school and made a killing doing that instead of wholesale. \n\nPlus, they always try to upsell you on those artisan beard oils and pomades that cost thirty bucks a pop. It's just a total racket if you ask me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the same as what other people think it is\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really know exactly what other people are thinking. I know they think I'm thin, like I said, but I don't know if the number in their head matches the number on the scale. \n\nBut talking about numbers... We've got this one supplier out in Ohio, and they used to guarantee a two-week turnaround on bulk orders. Now? You're lucky if you see it in six weeks. And they don't even call you to tell you it's delayed! You just have to sit there refreshing the tracking portal like an idiot. \n\nI had a client last week absolutely chewing me out because his shipment of industrial solvents was three weeks late. I went to this office supply place out by the highway, and you wouldn't believe the prices they're charging for a piece of mesh and some plastic wheels. They wanted almost eight hundred dollars for a chair! Eight hundred! \n\nAnd the guy working there, this kid who looked like he was barely out of high school, was trying to tell me about lumbar support technology like he's some kind of chiropractor. I asked him if it came with a warranty, and he actually laughed and said, \"Yeah, for an extra hundred bucks.\" I just walked right out. I ended up ordering one online, but now I have to wait three weeks for it to ship because it's coming from overseas. I've started taking my afternoon calls standing up in the kitchen just to get some relief.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is different from how others see it?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really know. They installed these new machines that are supposed to be faster, but they're so sensitive that if you put your bags down slightly off-center, the whole thing freezes up and starts flashing a red light. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don't know if I'd say I appear *fat* when they say that, but... I definitely don't feel like I look the way they think I do. Like, they'll say I'm too thin, but I still see the areas I need to work on. It's just... I don't know, it doesn't match up. 

But honestly, speaking of things not matching up, my electric bill this month was completely insane. I opened the envelope yesterday, and it was double what it was this time last year. Double! And I haven't even been running the heat that much because, like I said, I've been working from home and I usually just wear a sweater. I called the utility company, and of course, you get stuck in that automated phone tree for ten minutes before you can even talk to a human. 

When I finally got this woman on the line, she started talking about "peak usage hours" and "tier adjustments." I'm like, look, I'm just one guy in a townhouse, I'm not running a server farm in my basement. She said they could send a technician out to check the meter, but they charge an eighty-dollar dispatch fee if they don't find anything wrong. It's such a racket. They know you have no choice but to pay it because what are you going to do, live by candlelight? I swear, every time I turn around lately, someone is trying to nickel and dime me. It's exhausting just trying to keep track of it all.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean, normal is kind of subjective, right? I know everyone is making a huge deal about my weight lately, like it's some kind of crisis. But I still jog every morning, I work my entire territory, I'm hitting all my sales numbers... so it really doesn't feel like some medical emergency to me. I'm functioning just fine. 

Actually, speaking of working my territory, you would not believe the nightmare the drive has been this week. They decided to do roadwork on Interstate 84 right in the middle of the day. I drive a standard sedan, nothing crazy, but it still costs me over seventy bucks to fill the tank. 

And the worst part is, I was on my way to see this new manufacturing client out in the county, and I ended up being twenty minutes late. I hate being late. I had to sit in my car listening to sports talk radio just to keep from losing my mind. They were talking about the draft picks, which is a whole other joke, honestly... but yeah, I don't know why the city can't just do construction at night like everywhere else. It completely threw off my whole afternoon schedule.

I don't know about how I actually am, but... I know that when I look in the mirror, especially right before a big client meeting or a date, I just immediately zero in on my stomach and my face. If I think I look soft in those areas, I just feel like people are going to see me as sloppy or weak. You can't look soft in sales, people won't respect you. 

But man, speaking of dates, I actually went on one last Thursday, and the whole setup downtown is just a complete disaster right now. We went to that new bistro on Main Street, right? First of all, the city got rid of all the street parking to put in those protected bike lanes that literally nobody uses. I drive down there all the time for my accounts, and I never see a single bicycle. So I had to park in this garage four blocks away, and it's a twenty-five dollar flat rate. Twenty-five dollars just to leave my car there for two hours! 

And then we get inside the restaurant, and they don't even hand you a menu. It's just one of those little QR code stickers stuck to the table. You have to scan it with your phone, but the building is made of old brick, so there's absolutely zero cell service inside. I spent the first ten minutes of the date waving my phone in the air like a moron trying to get a signal just so I could see the drink list. I don't get why we can't just have normal, printed menus anymore. It feels like every business is cutting corners on basic service, but they still have the nerve to charge thirty bucks for a basic plate of chicken. It's unbelievable.

I don't really know if it's different. Like, I see what I see. I guess sometimes I wonder if I'm focusing too much on certain parts, but... I don't know, it's hard to tell what's real and what's just me being critical. 

But honestly, speaking of being critical, my landlord has been on my case all week about the recycling bins. We have these giant blue bins out behind the complex, and apparently, someone has been putting regular trash in them. So instead of, I don't know, figuring out who actually did it, he sends this mass email to the entire building threatening to raise everyone's utility fee if it happens again. Like, how is that legal? You can't just punish fifty people because one guy threw a pizza box in the wrong bin. 

And the worst part is, I actually go out of my way to break down all my cardboard boxes. I get a lot of packages because I order my protein powder and supplements online—it's just way cheaper than buying them at the store, especially with inflation right now. The prices at the local supplement shop have gone up like thirty percent since last year. I used to go in there and chat with the owner, this guy named Mike, but I just can't justify spending that kind of money anymore. Anyway, I break all my boxes down flat, so I know it's not me causing the problem. I tried calling the property manager to explain that, but of course, it went straight to voicemail. It's just so frustrating when you do everything right and still get lumped in with people who don't care.

I actually had to drive out to his facility in person to smooth things over. Took me two hours in traffic just to get there, and the AC in my car decided to stop working halfway there. Do you know how much it costs to fix an AC compressor on a German car? It's basically a mortgage payment. I'm dreading taking it into the shop.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look different in the mirror than you actually do in real life?\n\nPatient: I feel like we just talked about this, but yeah... I don't know if I look *different* exactly, but when I look in the mirror, especially right before a big client meeting or a date or something, I just immediately zero in on my stomach and my face. And if I think I look even a little bit soft, I just panic. I feel like people are going to see me and think I'm sloppy or weak, you know? Like I don't have my life together. \n\nBut speaking of having things together, I was trying to organize my home office over the weekend because I've been working from home a lot more lately. And I went to buy one of those ergonomic desk chairs, right? Because my back has been killing me from sitting on this cheap dining room chair for the last six months. I went to this office supply place out by the highway, and you wouldn't believe the prices they're charging for a piece of mesh and some plastic wheels. They wanted almost eight hundred dollars for a chair! Eight hundred! \n\nAnd the guy working there, this kid who looked like he was barely out of high school, was trying to tell me about lumbar support technology like he's some kind of chiropractor. I asked him if it came with a warranty, and he actually laughed and said, \"Yeah, for an extra hundred bucks.\" I just walked right out. I ended up ordering one online, but now I have to wait three weeks for it to ship because it's coming from overseas. They installed these new machines that are supposed to be faster, but they're so sensitive that if you put your bags down slightly off-center, the whole thing freezes up and starts flashing a red light. Then you have to stand there waiting for the one employee who's managing twelve registers to come over and scan her badge. I just wanted a bag of coffee and some apples, and it turned into a thirty-minute ordeal. I swear, the next time I need groceries, I'm just going to pay the delivery fee and let someone else deal with it. My time is worth more than the ten bucks they charge to bring it to my door.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you appear fat even when others tell you that you are underweight?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd say I appear *fat* when they say that, but... I definitely don't feel like I look the way they think I do. Like, they'll say I'm too thin, but I still see the areas I need to work on. It's just... I don't know, it doesn't match up. \n\nBut honestly, speaking of things not matching up, my electric bill this month was completely insane. I opened the envelope yesterday, and it was double what it was this time last year. Double! And I haven't even been running the heat that much because, like I said, I've been working from home and I usually just wear a sweater. I called the utility company, and of course, you get stuck in that automated phone tree for ten minutes before you can even talk to a human. \n\nWhen I finally got this woman on the line, she started talking about \"peak usage hours\" and \"tier adjustments.\" I'm like, look, I'm just one guy in a townhouse, I'm not running a server farm in my basement. She said they could send a technician out to check the meter, but they charge an eighty-dollar dispatch fee if they don't find anything wrong. It's such a racket. They know you have no choice but to pay it because what are you going to do, live by candlelight? I swear, every time I turn around lately, someone is trying to nickel and dime me. It's exhausting just trying to keep track of it all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is normal, even when others tell you it's not?\n\nPatient: I mean, normal is kind of subjective, right? I know everyone is making a huge deal about my weight lately, like it's some kind of crisis. But I still jog every morning, I work my entire territory, I'm hitting all my sales numbers... so it really doesn't feel like some medical emergency to me. I'm functioning just fine. \n\nActually, speaking of working my territory, you would not believe the nightmare the drive has been this week. They decided to do roadwork on Interstate 84 right in the middle of the day. Who does major paving at 1 PM on a Tuesday? They had it down to one lane for three miles. I drive a standard sedan, nothing crazy, but it still costs me over seventy bucks to fill the tank. \n\nAnd the worst part is, I was on my way to see this new manufacturing client out in the county, and I ended up being twenty minutes late.
